<td valign="top"><strong>Question:</strong> Which option best classifies the protein’s N-terminal targeting segment for the stated task?<br><br><strong>Choices:</strong><br><br><strong>A.</strong> A signal peptide that directs the nascent protein into the secretion pathway.<br><br><strong>B.</strong> A mitochondrial transit peptide that directs the protein to the mitochondrial matrix.<br><br><strong>C.</strong> A retained transmembrane anchor that keeps the mature protein embedded in a membrane.<br><br><strong>D.</strong> A propeptide that is removed to activate the protein after translation.<br><br><strong>Flash-Next response:</strong><br><br><br><br>1. reasoning: I analyzed the N-terminal region of this protein sequence (MNLKILVGLFILGIIILSAMTFLNFTTIVAQDKGD...) to identify the targeting segment type.<br><br>Key observations about the N-terminus:<br>- **N-region**: MNLK — contains a positively charged residue (K), typical of the n-region of a signal peptide<br>- **H-region**: ILVGLFILGIIILSAM — a strongly hydrophobic core (~16 residues), characteristic of the h-region of a signal peptide<br>- **C-region**: TFLNFTTIVAQDKGD — more polar residues with potential small amino acids near a cleavage site<br><br>This three-part structure (polar N-region → hydrophobic H-region → polar C-region) is the hallmark of a classical signal peptide.<br><br>Evaluating the other options:<br>- **B (mitochondrial transit peptide)**: These are enriched in Arg, Ser, Thr and lack a strong hydrophobic core — inconsistent with the observed sequence.<br>- **C (transmembrane anchor)**: A stable TM anchor would require a longer continuous hydrophobic stretch (~20+ residues) without a cleavage site; here the hydrophobic region is flanked by polar residues, consistent with a cleavable signal.<br>- **D (propeptide)**: Propeptides typically activate zymogens and don&#x27;t display the classical tripartite signal peptide architecture.<br><br>The remainder of the protein contains repeated motifs (GNT, GLY, GNG) suggestive of a ricin-like lectin, which are typically secreted proteins requiring an ER-targeting signal peptide.<br><br>2. answer: A</td>
